<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that witnessed symmetric choice strictly increases the expressive power of choiceless polynomial time with counting. A fixed sentence with one witnessed-choice occurrence defines a Boolean query on every finite input that is not definable in the original counting formalism.

- **claim-strict-expressive-separation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): There exist a vocabulary $\tau$ of eight binary relations, a fixed polynomial $p$, and a $\mathrm{CPT}+\mathrm{WSC}$ sentence $(\Phi,p)$ with one occurrence of a WSC operator such that:* 1. *$(\Phi,p)$ has a Boolean value on every finite $\tau$-structure;* 2. *no $\mathrm{CPT}$ sentence over $\tau$ has the same true-model class.* *The $\mathrm{CPT}$ sentences in <span class="upright">(ii)</span> may use counting and hereditarily finite sets of unrestricted finite rank.
  - Explanation: Witnessed symmetric choice defines a total invariant Boolean query beyond full CPT with counting, even when finite hereditary rank is unrestricted.
- **claim-charge-independent-size** (bound, [source](#stmt-str-size)): For every $L\ge2$ and every $b\in\mathbb F_3^{V_L}$, all edge and configuration blocks of $A_b$ are nonempty, and $$\label{str:size-formula} \|A_b\|=N_L= \sum_{e\in E_L}3^{2\|F(e)\|+1} +\sum_{v\in V_L}3^{2\|F(v)\|+d(v)-1}.$$ Thus $N_L$ is independent of $b$, and $$\|Y_e\|\le3^9,\qquad \|X_v\|\le3^{29},\qquad L^3\le N_L\le(3^{10}+3^{29})L^3.$$
  - Explanation: The charges change the local scalar equations without changing block cardinalities. Uniform local bounds make the total structure size proportional to box volume.
- **claim-central-extension-group** (construction, [source](#stmt-str-central-extension)): The multiplication in Equation <a href="#str:group-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="str:group-law">[str:group-law]</a> makes $H$ a finite group. Its identity is $(0,0)$ and $(a,k)^{-1}=(-a,-k)$. The subgroup $\{0\}\times K$, identified with $K$, is central, and projection onto $C_F$ is a surjective homomorphism with kernel $K$. With the convention $[h,h']=hh'h^{-1}(h')^{-1}$, one has $$\label{str:commutator} [(a,k),(a',k')] =\left(0,\sum_{f\in F}\omega(a_f,a'_f)c^f\right).$$
  - Explanation: The alternating correction defines a finite group whose central circulation subgroup receives the commutators of face translations.
- **claim-faithful-full-input-action** (construction, [source](#stmt-str-action)): For every $L\ge2$ and $b\in\mathbb F_3^{V_L}$, the maps $h_{a,k}$ define a faithful action of $H$ by automorphisms of the entire $\tau$-structure $A_b$. Every $h_{a,k}$ preserves each edge and configuration block setwise. For $k\in K$, the central action $h_{0,k}$ fixes all face vectors and adds $k_e$ to the scalar coordinate on edge $e$.
  - Explanation: The group acts faithfully on atoms and preserves the entire input vocabulary. Its central subgroup changes scalar coordinates while leaving face vectors fixed.
- **claim-exact-tree-completion-test** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-sen-tree-test)): Suppose $\operatorname{Check}(A)$ holds, $\alpha\in P$, and $\operatorname{Complete}(\alpha,x)$ holds. Then $\Phi_{\rm out}(\alpha,x)$ is true if and only if there exist atoms $t_v\in v$, for every $v\in\mathcal V$, and $s_e\in e$, for every $e\in\mathcal E$, such that $$\mathsf I(t_v,s_e)\quad\text{whenever }v\sim e, \qquad s_e\in x\quad\text{whenever }e\notin T.$$
  - Explanation: Configuration deletion on the spanning tree accepts exactly those complete non-tree selections that extend to consistent configurations throughout the checked input.
- **claim-history-preserving-choice-orbits** (theorem, [source](#stmt-sen-history)): For every input and every $\alpha\in P$, each choice set reached from $x=\emptyset$ is a nonempty orbit under the automorphisms fixing $\alpha$ and every preceding state. The corresponding value of $s_{\rm wit}$ witnesses that orbit using automorphisms of the full input. Every path reaches its first repeated state after at most $n+1$ transitions.
  - Explanation: The guard supplies a nonempty certified orbit at every reached state. Pointwise fixation of selected atoms preserves all preceding states, and the growing selection stabilizes within the stated transition bound.
- **claim-expanded-sentence-resource-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-sen-resources)): For every finite input, the expansion of Equation <a href="#sen:sentence" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sen:sentence">[sen:sentence]</a>, annotated by Equation <a href="#sen:polynomial" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sen:polynomial">[sen:polynomial]</a>, has the following properties. Every constructed set, including temporary sets before any filtering, has transitive size at most $p(n)$. Every ordinary iteration stabilizes within $p(n)$ steps, and every WSC path to first stabilization has at most $p(n)$ state labels.
  - Explanation: The fixed annotation bounds temporary objects before filtering as well as ordinary iterations and individual witnessed-choice paths.
- **claim-total-all-input-definition** (construction, [source](#stmt-sen-all-input)): The sentence $(\Phi,p)$ of Equations <a href="#sen:sentence" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sen:sentence">[sen:sentence]</a> and <a href="#sen:polynomial" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="sen:polynomial">[sen:polynomial]</a> is a fixed $\mathrm{CPT}+\mathrm{WSC}$ sentence over $\tau$. It evaluates to either true or false on every finite $\tau$-structure, never to $\dagger$, and its true-model class is isomorphism-invariant.* *More explicitly, it is true precisely when $P\ne\emptyset$ and there exists $\alpha\in P$ such that every terminal state of the guarded selection is complete and extends to consistent configurations as in Lemma <a href="#sen:tree-test" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sen:tree-test">5</a>.
  - Explanation: The guarded sentence returns a Boolean answer on every finite input. Its accepting inputs have a resolving parameter whose terminal selections are complete and consistently extendible.
- **claim-grid-resolving-parameters** (construction, [source](#stmt-grid-parameters)): Every structure $A_b$ passes the input check of Section <a href="#sec:sentence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:sentence">4</a>, its block graph is the box on $V_L$, and its parameter set $P$ is nonempty.
  - Explanation: The input relations recover the box graph, and distances from suitable corner blocks provide a resolving parameter tuple.
- **claim-face-coordinate-recovery** (theorem, [source](#stmt-grid-matching)): Every face description $\widehat f$ on $A_b$ describes an elementary face $f$, and every elementary face has such a description. For atoms $s,s'$ on any two sides of $f$, including the same side, $$\label{grid:match-equivalence} \operatorname{match}_{\widehat f}(s,s') \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad u_{s,f}=u_{s',f}.$$ Consequently, for $s=(e,u,z)$ and $s'=(e,u',z')$, $$\label{grid:base-equivalence} \operatorname{baseEq}_{Y_e}(s,s')\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad u=u'.$$
  - Explanation: Face comparisons recover equality of the vector attached to a face. Comparing over all faces of an edge recovers its entire vector pattern while allowing scalar differences.
- **claim-candidate-transport-with-history** (construction, [source](#stmt-grid-transitivity)): Let $A_b$ be a grid structure, $\alpha\in P$, and $x$ a compatible prefix of length $j<r$. For every ordered pair $s,s'\in D(\alpha,x)$ there is $g\in M(\alpha,x)$ with $g(s)=s'$. The map $g$ preserves the full input, fixes every block setwise, and fixes every atom of $x$ individually. Consequently every selection path for $\alpha$ chooses one atom from each edge outside $T$, in order, maintains compatibility, and then stabilizes with the dummy choice. All its witnessing maps respect $\alpha$ and the entire preceding history.
  - Explanation: A local vector translation followed by a tree-cycle circulation transports any candidate to any other candidate while fixing all previous selections and preserving the full input.
- **claim-unique-tree-divergence-solution** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-grid-tree-divergence)): Let $T$ be a finite tree on vertex set $V$, orient its edges arbitrarily, and write $\partial_T$ for its signed incidence map. Let $r\in\mathbb F_3^V$ satisfy $\sum_{v\in V}r_v=0$. There is a unique $z\in\mathbb F_3^{E(T)}$ with $\partial_T z=r$.
  - Explanation: Removing leaves forces each incident edge value and transfers the remaining demand to the neighbor. Total charge zero makes the final root equation consistent and yields uniqueness.
- **claim-opposite-grid-query-values** (theorem, [source](#stmt-grid-separation)): For every $L\ge2$, the fixed sentence $\Phi$ of Section <a href="#sec:sentence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:sentence">4</a> evaluates to true on $A_{b^0}$ and to false on $A_{b^1}$. For either structure, every parameter $\alpha\in P$ and every terminal selection gives the stated output value, and no evaluation produces $\dagger$.
  - Explanation: Every compatible terminal selection extends on the zero-charge structure. On the unit-charge structure, summing all vertex equations contradicts cancellation along edges.
- **claim-sparse-cochain-representative** (construction, [source](#stmt-sup-sparse-cochain)): Let $\ell\in\mathbb F_3^E$, and let $t=\|\{f\in F:(D\ell)(f)\ne0\}\|$. There is a vertex function $\varphi\in\mathbb F_3^V$ such that $$\|\operatorname{supp}(\ell-\partial^{\mathsf T}\varphi)\|\le2Lt, \qquad \operatorname{supp}(r)=\{e\in E:r_e\ne0\}.$$ In particular, every cochain with zero curl is a gradient.
  - Explanation: Successive gradient subtractions eliminate coordinate directions and concentrate the remaining cochain near nonzero curls. In particular, zero curl leaves no residual cochain.
- **claim-small-orbit-central-support** (bound, [source](#stmt-sup-small-orbit)): Let $L\ge2$, $b\in\mathbb F_3^{V_L}$, and $N=\|A_b\|$. Let $q\ge0$ and let $x$ be an atom or a hereditarily finite set over $A_b$. If $\|H\cdot x\|\le N^q$, then $x$ has a $K$-support consisting of at most $$2L(q\log_3N)^2$$ edge atoms. There is no bound on the finite rank of $x$ in this statement.
  - Explanation: A polynomially bounded orbit under the full group forces a short support for the central subgroup. The argument uses stabilizers and commutators rather than induction on rank.
- **claim-hereditary-supported-invariant-family** (bound, [source](#stmt-sup-hereditary)): Let $L\ge2$, $b\in\mathbb F_3^{V_L}$, and $N=\|A_b\|$. Suppose $\mathcal X$ is a finite $H$-invariant, membership-transitive family of atoms and hereditarily finite sets over $A_b$, with $\|\mathcal X\|\le N^q$ for some $q\ge0$. Every member of $\mathcal X$ and every recursive member has a $K$-support of length at most $2L(q\log_3N)^2$. In particular, every member is hereditarily $s$-supported for $$\label{sup:length} s=\left\lceil2L(q\log_3N+1)^2\right\rceil+1.$$ All the supports may be padded to length exactly $s$. For fixed $q$, $$s=O_q\bigl(L(1+\log L)^2\bigr).$$
  - Explanation: Invariance places each orbit inside the bounded family, and membership transitivity extends the same support conclusion to every recursive member. Supports can then be padded to a common length.
- **claim-box-boundary-lower-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-base-boundary)): Let $c=1-2^{-1/3}$. For every $L\ge2$ and every nonempty $R\subseteq\{0,\ldots,L-1\}^3$ with $\|R\|\le L^3/2$, $$\|\delta_G R\|\ge c\|R\|^{2/3}.$$
  - Explanation: A large coordinate projection supplies many occupied lines. Removing lines wholly inside the vertex set leaves enough distinct boundary edges to prove the stated lower bound.
- **claim-persistent-giant-component** (theorem, [source](#stmt-base-giant)): For all sufficiently large $L$, every graph $G\setminus T$ obtained by deleting at most $6M$ edges has a unique component with more than $L^3/2$ vertices. If $T\subseteq T'$ and $\|T'\|\le6M$, the component for $T'$ is contained in the component for $T$.
  - Explanation: The boundary estimate bounds the total volume of small components after the permitted deletions. A unique majority component remains and nests under further deletion.
- **claim-base-counting-equivalence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-base-equivalence)): For all sufficiently large $L$, the expansions $\bar A_{b^0}$ and $\bar A_{b^1}$ satisfy the same $C^M$ sentences, where $b^0=0$, $b^1=\mathbf1_{v_*}$, and $M$ is as in <a href="#base:M" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="base:M">[base:M]</a>.
  - Explanation: Whole-universe bijections move an unmatched charge inside the giant component while preserving the currently assigned atoms. Formula induction then preserves exact counts.
- **claim-partial-circulation-extension** (algorithm, [source](#stmt-base-partial-flow)): Let $T\subseteq E$ and $\sigma\in\mathbb F_3^T$. There is a vector $k\in\ker\partial$ with $k\|_T=\sigma$ if and only if every component $U$ of $G\setminus T$ satisfies $$\label{base:balance} R_U:=\sum_{v\in U}\sum_{\substack{e\in T\\e\ni v}} \epsilon_{ve}\sigma_e=0.$$ If no extension exists, some nonempty component violating <a href="#base:balance" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="base:balance">[base:balance]</a> has $$\|U\|\le L^3/2, \qquad \|U\|\le(\|T\|/c)^{3/2}.$$
  - Explanation: A prescribed edge shift extends to a circulation exactly when each remaining component has balanced total demand. Any obstruction has a small component witness controlled by the boundary estimate.
- **claim-quantitative-central-homogeneity** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-base-homogeneity)): Let $L\ge2$, $b\in\mathbb F_3^V$, and let $r,M$ be positive integers such that $$\label{base:homogeneity-bound} 7r+7(6r/c)^{3/2}+1\le M.$$ If two $r$-tuples in $\bar A_b$ have the same $C^M$-type, an element of $K$ maps the first tuple to the second. Empty tuples lie in the same $K$-orbit without any width hypothesis.
  - Explanation: Equal counting types determine compatible scalar shifts on touched edges. The variable budget can express every possible component obstruction, forcing the shift to extend to a central automorphism.
- **claim-support-scale-fits-counting-width** (bound, [source](#stmt-base-width)): Fix positive integers $q,m$ independently of $L$, and let $$N=N_L=\Theta(L^3),\qquad s=\left\lceil2L(q\log_3N+1)^2\right\rceil+1, \qquad M=\lfloor L^{7/4}\rfloor.$$ For all sufficiently large $L$, Proposition <a href="#base:equivalence" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="base:equivalence">20</a> holds, Proposition <a href="#base:homogeneity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="base:homogeneity">22</a> applies to every tuple length $1\le r\le(m+4)s$ in either expansion, and $$\max\{4,m+1\}s\le M.$$ In particular, both expansions are $K$-homogeneous for equal $C^M$ types of tuples of length at most $\max\{2,m\}s$.
  - Explanation: After the sentence parameters are fixed, the support scale grows slowly enough for the base equivalence, homogeneity, and transfer-width requirements to hold eventually.
- **claim-rank-independent-supported-hf-transfer** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-hf-transfer)): Let $B_0,B_1$ be nonempty finite structures in the same finite relational vocabulary of arity at most two, and let $K_i\leq\operatorname{Aut}(B_i)$ for $i=0,1$. Let $s,m,M$ be positive integers with $$\max\{4,m+1\}s\leq M.$$ Suppose that:* 1. *$B_0$ and $B_1$ satisfy the same $C^M$ sentences;* 2. *in each $B_i$, any two tuples of length at most $\max\{2,m\}s$ having the same $C^M$ type belong to the same $K_i$-orbit.* *Then $D_s(B_0,K_0)$ and $D_s(B_1,K_1)$ satisfy the same $C^m$ sentences in membership, atomhood, and the base relations.
  - Explanation: Base counting equivalence and orbit homogeneity transfer to the entire hereditarily supported domains. The conclusion preserves counting without imposing a rank bound.
- **claim-exact-type-extension-counts** (theorem, [source](#stmt-hf-extension-counts)): Let $P,P'$ be tuples of the same length $a$, in two structures chosen from $B_0,B_1$, allowing the two structures to be the same. Suppose that $P,P'$ have the same $C^M$ type. If $a+d\leq M$, then for every realized type $\rho$ of length $a+d$, the numbers of tuples $\gamma,\gamma'$ of length $d$ satisfying $$\operatorname{tp}_M(P,\gamma)=\rho, \qquad \operatorname{tp}_M(P',\gamma')=\rho$$ are equal. Equal types also give equal types on every projected or reordered subtuple.
  - Explanation: A realized type can be isolated over the finite bases. Exact counting for successive coordinates preserves the number of tuple extensions, including absent extensions.
- **claim-supported-object-form-representation** (construction, [source](#stmt-hf-representation)): Every value $\phi*\alpha$ lies in $D_i$ and is supported by $\alpha$. Every object of $D_i$ has a representation of this kind. Furthermore, for each $g\in K_i$, $$g(\phi*\alpha)=\phi*(g\alpha).$$
  - Explanation: Finite forms evaluated at supporting atom tuples represent exactly the hereditarily supported objects. Evaluation commutes with the group action.
- **claim-form-atomic-facts-transfer** (theorem, [source](#stmt-hf-atomic-transfer)): Let $\phi,\psi$ be forms, and let $(\alpha,\beta)$ and $(\alpha',\beta')$ be pairs of molecules having the same $C^M$ type, in the same or different base structures. Then $$\begin{aligned} \phi*\alpha=\psi*\beta &\ \Longleftrightarrow\ \phi*\alpha'=\psi*\beta',\\ \phi*\alpha\in\psi*\beta &\ \Longleftrightarrow\ \phi*\alpha'\in\psi*\beta'. \end{aligned}$$ Atomhood and all base relations also agree on the corresponding values.
  - Explanation: Matching joint molecule types preserve equality, membership, atomhood, and base relations between form values. The proof's variable requirement does not depend on form depth.
- **claim-supported-domain-extension-bijection** (construction, [source](#stmt-hf-bijection)): For represented-alike assignments with $r\leq m-1$, there is a bijection $f:D_0\to D_1$ such that adjoining $x,f(x)$ gives represented-alike assignments for every $x\in D_0$. Moreover, $f(x_j)=x'_j$ for every retained position $j$.
  - Explanation: Matching label orbits have equal value-set cardinalities after accounting for repeated representations. Their finite bijections combine into a domain bijection compatible with retained assignments.
- **claim-polynomial-invariant-evaluation-trace** (bound, [source](#stmt-eval-trace)): For every fixed CPT sentence $\psi$, there is an integer $q\ge1$ such that, on every sufficiently large finite input $A$ of size $n$, $$\|\mathcal T_\psi(A)\|\le n^q.$$ The family $\mathcal T_\psi(A)$ is membership-transitive and invariant under $\operatorname{Aut}(A)$. It includes the first excessive candidate of each activated iteration and, when needed, its candidate at index $P(n)+1$.
  - Explanation: The actual evaluation family remains polynomially bounded and invariant even when rejected candidates and the final stationarity candidate are included.
- **claim-fixed-width-exact-evaluation-description** (construction, [source](#stmt-eval-description)): Fix a CPT sentence $\psi$. There are an integer $m\ge1$ and, for each sufficiently large input size $n$, a $C^m$ sentence $\widehat\psi_n$ with the following property.* *Let $A$ be an $n$-element input and let $D\subseteq\operatorname{HF}(A)$ be membership-transitive, contain all atoms and all pure HF sets, and contain $\mathcal T_\psi(A)$. Interpret membership and the atom predicate on $D$, and interpret each input relation as false if an argument is a set. Then $$D\models\widehat\psi_n \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad \psi\text{ evaluates to true on }A.$$ The formula $\widehat\psi_n$ depends only on $\psi$ and $n$, not on $A$ or $D$. Its length and counting constants may depend on $n$; $m$ does not.
  - Explanation: For each common input size, a counting sentence of fixed variable width describes the actual CPT answer. Membership closure and output uniqueness prevent unrelated objects from creating spurious continuations or suppressing defaults.
- **claim-eventual-cpt-pair-equivalence** (theorem, [source](#stmt-eval-pair-equivalence)): For every sentence $\psi$ of full CPT with counting over $\tau$, there is $L_\psi$ such that for every $L\ge L_\psi$, $$A_{b^0}\models\psi \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad A_{b^1}\models\psi .$$
  - Explanation: For each fixed CPT sentence, sufficiently large charged grid pairs receive identical answers. Supported-domain transfer applies to the common fixed-width description of their actual evaluations.

        "anchor": "stmt-grid-transitivity",
        "exact_quote": "Let $A_b$ be a grid structure, $\\alpha\\in P$, and $x$ a compatible prefix of length $j<r$. For every ordered pair $s,s'\\in D(\\alpha,x)$ there is $g\\in M(\\alpha,x)$ with $g(s)=s'$. The map $g$ preserves the full input, fixes every block setwise, and fixes every atom of $x$ individually. Consequently every selection path for $\\alpha$ chooses one atom from each edge outside $T$, in order, maintains compatibility, and then stabilizes with the dummy choice.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-sup-hereditary",
        "exact_quote": "Let $L\\ge2$, $b\\in\\mathbb F_3^{V_L}$, and $N=|A_b|$. Suppose $\\mathcal X$ is a finite $H$-invariant, membership-transitive family of atoms and hereditarily finite sets over $A_b$, with $|\\mathcal X|\\le N^q$ for some $q\\ge0$. Every member of $\\mathcal X$ and every recursive member has a $K$-support of length at most $2L(q\\log_3N)^2$. In particular, every member is hereditarily $s$-supported for $$\\label{sup:length}\n s=\\left\\lceil2L(q\\log_3N+1)^2\\right\\rceil+1.$$ All the supports may be padded to length exactly $s$. For fixed $q$, $$s=O_q\\bigl(L(1+\\log L)^2\\bigr).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-243.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-base-width",
        "exact_quote": "Fix positive integers $q,m$ independently of $L$, and let $$N=N_L=\\Theta(L^3),\\qquad\n s=\\left\\lceil2L(q\\log_3N+1)^2\\right\\rceil+1,\n \\qquad M=\\lfloor L^{7/4}\\rfloor.$$ For all sufficiently large $L$, Proposition <a href=\"#base:equivalence\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"base:equivalence\">20</a> holds, Proposition <a href=\"#base:homogeneity\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"base:homogeneity\">22</a> applies to every tuple length $1\\le r\\le(m+4)s$ in either expansion, and $$\\max\\{4,m+1\\}s\\le M.$$ In particular, both expansions are $K$-homogeneous for equal $C^M$ types of tuples of length at most $\\max\\{2,m\\}s$.",
        "source_id": "PM-243.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-base-width",
          "exact_quote": "Fix positive integers $q,m$ independently of $L$, and let $$N=N_L=\\Theta(L^3),\\qquad\n s=\\left\\lceil2L(q\\log_3N+1)^2\\right\\rceil+1,\n \\qquad M=\\lfloor L^{7/4}\\rfloor.$$ For all sufficiently large $L$, Proposition <a href=\"#base:equivalence\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"base:equivalence\">20</a> holds, Proposition <a href=\"#base:homogeneity\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"base:homogeneity\">22</a> applies to every tuple length $1\\le r\\le(m+4)s$ in either expansion, and $$\\max\\{4,m+1\\}s\\le M.$$ In particular, both expansions are $K$-homogeneous for equal $C^M$ types of tuples of length at most $\\max\\{2,m\\}s$.",
          "source_id": "PM-243.2"
        }
      ],

        "exact_quote": "Let $B_0,B_1$ be nonempty finite structures in the same finite relational vocabulary of arity at most two, and let $K_i\\leq\\operatorname{Aut}(B_i)$ for $i=0,1$.       "explanation": "For each fixed CPT sentence, sufficiently large charged grid pairs receive identical answers. Supported-domain transfer applies to the common fixed-width description of their actual evaluations.",
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "hypotheses": [
        {

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Hereditarily finite universe over atoms** ([source](#pm-h-6)): For a finite set $A$ of atoms, $\operatorname{HF}(A)$ contains the atoms and all hereditarily finite sets built from them.
- **Strict transitive closure** ([source](#pm-h-6)): For a set $a$, let $\operatorname{TC}(a)$ be the set of all objects reachable from $a$ by a positive-length downward membership chain.
- **Witnessing condition for a choice orbit** ([source](#mod:witness-condition)): A set $M_i$ of permutation graphs witnesses a nonempty choice set $D_i$ at stage $i$ if $$M_i\subseteq\operatorname{Aut}(A,\bar a,b_0,\ldots,b_i) \quad\text{and}\quad \forall u,v\in D_i\ \exists g\in M_i\ (g(u)=v). \tag{*}\label{mod:witness-condition}$$
- **Resolving tuple of vertex blocks** ([source](#pm-h-14)): A tuple $\alpha=(a_1,a_2,a_3,a_4)\in\mathcal V^4$ is *resolving* if the map $$v\longmapsto (d(a_1,v),d(a_2,v),d(a_3,v),d(a_4,v))$$ is injective on $\mathcal V$.
- **Compatible selection prefix** ([source](#pm-h-26)): Say that $x$ is a compatible prefix of length $j$ if it consists of one atom on each of $e_1,\ldots,e_j$ and no other elements, and if its atoms have equal $f$-vectors whenever their edges belong to a common face $f$.
- **Central support** ([source](#stmt-sup-definition)): For an atom tuple $\alpha=(\alpha_1,\ldots,\alpha_r)$, let $$K_\alpha=\{k\in K:k\cdot\alpha_j=\alpha_j \text{ for every }1\le j\le r\}.$$ The tuple $\alpha$ is a *$K$-support* of an atom or hereditarily finite set $x$ over $A_b$ if every element of $K_\alpha$ fixes $x$. The empty tuple is permitted, and $K_{()}=K$.
- **Edge cochain** ([source](#pm-h-29)): An *edge cochain* is a vector $\ell\in\mathbb F_3^E$.
- **Curl of an edge cochain** ([source](#pm-h-29)): Define its curl $D\ell\in\mathbb F_3^F$ by $$(D\ell)(f)=\sum_{e\in E}c^f_e\ell_e.$$
- **Membership-transitive family** ([source](#pm-h-31)): A family $\mathcal X$ of atoms and hereditarily finite sets is *membership-transitive* if $y\in x\in\mathcal X$ implies $y\in\mathcal X$.
- **Hereditary support bound** ([source](#pm-h-31)): We call an object *hereditarily $s$-supported* if it and every such recursive member have a $K$-support of length at most $s$; these supports need not coincide.
- **Counting logic with bounded variable names** ([source](#pm-h-33)): For a positive integer $h$, let $C^h$ be first-order logic with equality, ordinary quantifiers, and exact counting quantifiers $\exists^{=a}x$, using at most $h$ distinct variable names.
- **Molecule** ([source](#pm-h-39)): Call a tuple in $B_i^s$ a *molecule*.
- **Evaluation activation** ([source](#pm-h-42)): An *activation* is a syntax occurrence together with the values assigned to its free variables when that occurrence is evaluated.
- **Complete actual evaluation family** ([source](#pm-h-42)): Let $\mathcal T_\psi(A)$ be the membership closure of all arguments and term values occurring in these activations, together with all input atoms and $\emptyset$.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-edge-block-size | \|Y_e\| | ≤ | $3^9$ | Uniform over box size, charge, and edge. |
| q-configuration-block-size | \|X_v\| | ≤ | $3^{29}$ | Uniform over box size, charge, and vertex. |
| q-universe-volume-lower-bound | N_L | ≥ | $L^3$ | The cardinality is independent of the charge vector. |
| q-universe-volume-upper-bound | N_L | ≤ | $(3^{10}+3^{29})L^3$ | Uniform over charge vectors and box sizes. |
| q-local-permutation-budget | B | = | $16(3^9+3^{29})$ | A fixed syntax constant, independent of the input. |
| q-choice-path-transition-bound | Transitions to the first repeated state | ≤ | $n+1$ | The bound applies to every reachable choice path. |
| q-expanded-resource-polynomial | p(n) | = | $(n+2)^{d_{H_0+1}}$ | The expansion and its syntax height are fixed before input size varies. |
| q-sparse-cochain-support | \|\operatorname{supp}(\ell-\partial^{\mathsf T}\varphi)\| | ≤ | $2Lt$ | The curl support determines the stated residual support bound. |
| q-small-orbit-support-length | Length of a central support in edge atoms | ≤ | $2L(q\log_3N)^2$ | The estimate is independent of finite hereditary rank. |
| q-padded-hereditary-support-length | s | = | $\left\lceil2L(q\log_3N+1)^2\right\rceil+1$ | The common length permits padding of each separate support. |
| q-box-boundary-constant | c | = | $1-2^{-1/3}$ | A constant independent of the box size and the vertex set. |
| q-box-edge-boundary | \|\delta_G R\| | ≥ | $c\|R\|^{2/3}$ | The vertex set must be nonempty and contain at most half the vertices. |
| q-base-counting-variable-budget | M | = | $\lfloor L^{7/4}\rfloor$ | The budget varies with box size; its eventual uses require sufficiently large boxes. |
| q-obstruction-component-size | \|U\| | ≤ | $(\|T\|/c)^{3/2}$ | The bound concerns a component violating the circulation balance condition. |
| q-homogeneity-width-requirement | 7r+7(6r/c)^{3/2}+1 | ≤ | $M$ | A sufficient width hypothesis for comparing tuples of the indicated length. |
| q-supported-transfer-width-requirement | \max\{4,m+1\}s | ≤ | $M$ | This numerical requirement accompanies base counting equivalence and orbit homogeneity. |
| q-polynomial-actual-evaluation-family | \|\mathcal T_\psi(A)\| | ≤ | $n^q$ | The exponent is chosen after fixing the CPT sentence and its annotation. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-count-structure-blocks** [claim](#stmt-str-size) / [proof](#proof-stmt-str-size): Count independent face vectors and solve the remaining scalar equation at each vertex. -> Nonempty blocks, charge-independent universe size, and uniform block bounds.
- **step-build-central-extension** [claim](#stmt-str-central-extension) / [proof](#proof-stmt-str-central-extension): Use bilinearity for associativity and alternation for inverses and the commutator formula. -> A finite central extension with the specified commutators.
- **step-prove-full-input-action** [claim](#stmt-str-action) / [proof](#proof-stmt-str-action): Identify edge transformations with left multiplication, check configuration divergence, and preserve corrected group differences. -> A faithful block-preserving automorphism action.
- **step-prove-tree-test-exact** [claim](#stmt-sen-tree-test) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sen-tree-test): Show consistent configurations survive deletion; construct a global completion from survivors by traversing the tree. -> Equivalence between the output test and a consistent completion.
- **step-certify-choice-history** [claim](#stmt-sen-history) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sen-history): Induct on growing selection prefixes and use the filtered maps and transitivity guard to identify each choice set with its stabilizer orbit. -> Legal history-preserving witnesses and bounded first stabilization.
- **step-bound-expanded-resources** [claim](#stmt-sen-resources) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sen-resources): Bound iteration states independently, then induct on expanded syntax height to bound every temporary value before cutoffs. -> No ordinary or witnessed-choice resource cutoff.
- **step-establish-total-sentence** [claim](#stmt-sen-all-input) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sen-all-input): Combine legal witnesses, sufficient resources, exact tree completion, and equivariance of the outer parameter comprehension. -> A fixed invariant Boolean query on all finite inputs.
- **step-resolve-grid-vertices** [claim](#stmt-grid-parameters) / [proof](#proof-stmt-grid-parameters): Recover the box through block incidence and use corner-distance differences to determine vertex coordinates. -> Successful structural checks and a nonempty resolving-parameter set.
- **step-recover-face-vectors** [claim](#stmt-grid-matching) / [proof](#proof-stmt-grid-matching): Classify simple square cycles and extend compatible adjacent edge atoms to a vertex configuration using an unprescribed scalar coordinate. -> Exact recovery of face-vector equality and full edge-vector equality.
- **step-transport-grid-candidates** [claim](#stmt-grid-transitivity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-grid-transitivity): Correct differing face vectors by a bounded local translation, then correct the scalar discrepancy along the new edge and its tree return path. -> Transitivity of the filtered witness pool while fixing the whole history.
- **step-solve-tree-divergence** [claim](#stmt-grid-tree-divergence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-grid-tree-divergence): Remove a leaf, force its incident edge value, and transfer its demand to its neighbor. -> The unique tree-edge flow with the required divergence.
- **step-separate-grid-answers** [claim](#stmt-grid-separation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-grid-separation): Extend compatible vectors face by face and solve the remaining tree divergence for zero charge; sum vertex equations to refute a unit-charge completion. -> Opposite Boolean answers on every grid pair.
- **step-sparsify-cochains** [claim](#stmt-sup-sparse-cochain) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sup-sparse-cochain): Subtract coordinate antiderivatives, select a slice with few nonzero curls, and bound deviations from the copied slice values. -> A gradient-equivalent sparse cochain and exactness of zero-curl cochains.
- **step-extract-central-support** [claim](#stmt-sup-small-orbit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sup-small-orbit): Relate stabilizer index to quotient codimensions, use commutators to force isotropy, and sparsify annihilator representatives. -> A rank-independent short support in edge atoms.
- **step-make-supports-hereditary** [claim](#stmt-sup-hereditary) / [proof](#proof-stmt-sup-hereditary): Place each orbit inside the invariant family, use membership closure for descendants, and pad the supporting tuples. -> A common hereditary support-length bound.
- **step-bound-box-boundary** [claim](#stmt-base-boundary) / [proof](#proof-stmt-base-boundary): Apply the projection inequality and count occupied coordinate lines that are not wholly contained in the vertex set. -> The stated finite-box edge-boundary lower bound.
- **step-preserve-giant-component** [claim](#stmt-base-giant) / [proof](#proof-stmt-base-giant): Sum boundary estimates over small components and show their combined volume is eventually negligible. -> A unique majority component nested under further deletions.
- **step-transfer-base-counting** [claim](#stmt-base-equivalence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-base-equivalence): Move the unmatched charge within the giant component and combine blockwise shifts into whole-universe extension bijections. -> Eventual base counting equivalence.
- **step-extend-partial-flows** [claim](#stmt-base-partial-flow) / [proof](#proof-stmt-base-partial-flow): Solve divergence separately on the remaining components and use boundary expansion to bound a failing component. -> An exact extension criterion and a bounded obstruction witness.
- **step-establish-central-homogeneity** [claim](#stmt-base-homogeneity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-base-homogeneity): Recover consistent scalar shifts from tuple types and express any unbalanced component as a distinguishing counting formula. -> A central automorphism transporting the tuples under the width hypothesis.
- **step-fit-support-widths** [claim](#stmt-base-width) / [proof](#proof-stmt-base-width): Compare the fixed-parameter support scale and homogeneity requirements with the available counting width. -> All base and transfer hypotheses hold eventually after fixing the sentence parameters.
- **step-count-type-extensions** [claim](#stmt-hf-extension-counts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hf-extension-counts): Isolate realized finite-base type classes by formulas and induct on the number of added tuple coordinates. -> Equal exact extension counts and preservation under tuple projections.
- **step-represent-supported-objects** [claim](#stmt-hf-representation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hf-representation): Induct on forms for equivariance and on hereditary construction for completeness, using homogeneity to recover the entire represented set. -> Representations of all supported objects with no depth restriction.
- **step-transfer-form-atomic-facts** [claim](#stmt-hf-atomic-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hf-atomic-transfer): Induct on form depths for equality, transfer membership witnesses by type extension, and read atomhood and base relations from the forms and molecule types. -> Preservation of equality, membership, atomhood, and base relations.
- **step-build-supported-domain-bijections** [claim](#stmt-hf-bijection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hf-bijection): Partition labels into stabilizer orbits, compare overlaps and constant evaluation-fibre sizes, and combine equal-cardinality finite matches. -> A bijection of the whole supported domains preserving extension representations.
- **step-transfer-supported-counting** [claim](#stmt-hf-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-hf-bijection-2): Induct on counting formulas, using atomic transfer and an extension bijection after each variable is released. -> Counting equivalence of supported HF domains at every finite rank.
- **step-bound-actual-evaluation-family** [claim](#stmt-eval-trace) / [proof](#proof-stmt-eval-trace): Induct on syntax using polynomial hereditary argument sizes, retaining rejected candidates and the last stationarity candidate, then close under membership. -> A polynomially bounded invariant membership-transitive evaluation family.
- **step-describe-actual-evaluation** [claim](#stmt-eval-description) / [proof](#proof-stmt-eval-description): Build exact relational graphs for primitives, comprehensions, size guards, and bounded iteration, recycling variable names through size-dependent expansions. -> A common size-dependent evaluation sentence with fixed variable width and unique outputs at actual arguments.
- **step-prove-eventual-cpt-equivalence** [claim](#stmt-eval-pair-equivalence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-eval-pair-equivalence): Place both actual traces in supported domains and apply counting transfer to their common evaluation description. -> Equal CPT answers on every sufficiently large grid pair.
- **step-conclude-strict-expressive-separation** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-eval-pair-equivalence-2): Contrast the separating sentence's opposite grid answers with eventual equality for every fixed CPT sentence, then use syntactic inclusion. -> Strict inclusion of the definable Boolean-query collections.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-tree-divergence**: On a finite oriented tree, a divergence demand with zero total sum has a unique edge assignment over the stated finite field.
- **invariant-history-preserving-choice**: Every reachable choice set is a nonempty orbit witnessed by full-input automorphisms that preserve the parameters and preceding states.
- **invariant-opposite-grid-answers**: The fixed witnessed-choice sentence accepts the zero-charge grids and rejects the unit-charge grids, independently of the resolving parameter and terminal selection.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-nonzero-total-demand**: Apply the tree solver to a divergence demand with nonzero total sum. -> Summing signed edge contributions cancels every edge, so such a demand cannot be realized.
- **anti-cyclic-uniqueness**: Treat the unique tree-flow conclusion as a uniqueness theorem on a graph with cycles. -> Cycle circulations can alter edge assignments without changing their divergence.
- **anti-unchecked-symmetric-choice**: Select an arbitrary candidate after checking only local compatibility. -> Local compatibility does not certify transitivity under full-input automorphisms preserving the computation history.
- **anti-finite-tests-prove-separation**: Use successful tree-flow tests as a certificate of the full expressive separation. -> The finite solver does not establish hereditary support, counting transfer, legality of every witnessed-choice path, or eventual indistinguishability for arbitrary fixed CPT sentences.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The executable component implements the leaf-elimination construction of the finite tree-divergence lemma. It checks this constructive ingredient only; the full expressiveness separation also requires rank-independent supports, counting transfer, and eventual equivalence for arbitrary CPT sentences.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON object with n, oriented edges [tail, head], and vertex demands r.
# PRECONDITIONS: A nonempty finite tree; demands are residues modulo 3 and sum to zero modulo 3.
# OUTPUTS: JSON list of edge residues in input order, or an explicit precondition error.
# TIME: One leaf elimination per tree edge, after validating the graph.
# SPACE: Vertex adjacency lists, demands, degrees, and an edge output list.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact integer arithmetic reduced modulo 3.
# FAILURE: Malformed input or violated preconditions produces a precondition error.
import json
import sys
from collections import deque


def solve(data):
    fail = {'error': 'precondition'}
    if not isinstance(data, dict) or set(data) != {'n', 'edges', 'r'}:
        return fail
    n, edges, demand = data['n'], data['edges'], data['r']
    if type(n) is not int or n < 1:
        return fail
    if not isinstance(edges, list) or len(edges) != n - 1:
        return fail
    if not isinstance(demand, list) or len(demand) != n:
        return fail
    if any(type(a) is not int or not 0 <= a < 3 for a in demand):
        return fail
    if sum(demand) % 3 != 0:
        return fail
    adj = [[] for _ in range(n)]
    seen_edges = set()
    for idx, edge in enumerate(edges):
        if not isinstance(edge, list) or len(edge) != 2:
            return fail
        u, v = edge
        if type(u) is not int or type(v) is not int:
            return fail
        if not (0 <= u < n and 0 <= v < n) or u == v:
            return fail
        key = tuple(sorted((u, v)))
        if key in seen_edges:
            return fail
        seen_edges.add(key)
        adj[u].append((v, idx))
        adj[v].append((u, idx))
    reached = {0}
    stack = [0]
    while stack:
        u = stack.pop()
        for v, _ in adj[u]:
            if v not in reached:
                reached.add(v)
                stack.append(v)
    if len(reached) != n:
        return fail
    need = demand[:]
    degree = [len(row) for row in adj]
    active = [True] * len(edges)
    queue = deque(v for v in range(n) if degree[v] == 1)
    z = [0] * len(edges)
    remaining = len(edges)
    while queue:
        v = queue.popleft()
        if degree[v] != 1:
            continue
        w, idx = next((w, idx) for w, idx in adj[v] if active[idx])
        eps = -1 if edges[idx][0] == v else 1
        z[idx] = (eps * need[v]) % 3
        need[w] = (need[w] + need[v]) % 3
        need[v] = 0
        active[idx] = False
        remaining -= 1
        degree[v] -= 1
        degree[w] -= 1
        if degree[w] == 1:
            queue.append(w)
    if remaining != 0 or any(need):
        return fail
    return z


try:
    result = solve(json.load(sys.stdin))
except (ValueError, TypeError, KeyError, StopIteration):
    result = {'error': 'precondition'}
print(json.dumps(result, separators=(',', ':')))
```

            "exact_quote": "Define the bounded formula $$\\operatorname{Trans}(\\alpha,x)\n \\quad\\Longleftrightarrow\\quad\n \\forall s,s'\\in D(\\alpha,x)\\;\n   \\exists g\\in M(\\alpha,x)\\;\n                    \\langle s,s'\\rangle\\in g,$$ and the choice term $$s_{\\rm choice}(\\alpha,x)=\n \\begin{cases}\n D(\\alpha,x),&\n   D(\\alpha,x)\\ne\\emptyset\\ \\land\\operatorname{Trans}(\\alpha,x),\\\\\n \\{\\emptyset\\},&\\text{otherwise}.\n \\end{cases}\n \\label{sen:guarded-choice}$$ The singleton $\\{\\emptyset\\}$ supplies a pure dummy choice. The step and witness terms are $$\\begin{split}\n s_{\\rm step}(\\alpha,x,y)\n      &=x\\cup\\bigl(\\{y\\}\\cap Y\\bigr),\\\\\n s_{\\rm wit}(\\alpha,z,x)&=M(\\alpha,x).\n \\end{split}\n \\label{sen:step-witness}$$ In the second line $z$ denotes the resulting fixed point and $x$ denotes the intermediate state being certified. These are precisely the two slots, in this order, of the witnessing term in (Lichter and Schweitzer 2023, sec. 3.1) [[ls2023](#ref-ls2023)]; the final-state slot is unused. Equivalently the syntactic term supplied in the slots $(\\alpha,x,y)$ is $M(\\alpha,y)$. Unused formal variables may be inserted through tautological self-equalities if required. Under the member-choice convention, every real choice $y$ is an atom of $Y$, so the first line adds exactly that atom. The pure dummy $\\emptyset$ is not in $Y$ and causes no change.",

1 and len(r) == n\n    valid = valid and all(type(a) is int and a in (0, 1, 2) for a in r)\nif valid:\n    valid = sum(r) % 3 == 0\n    pairs = []\n    for edge in edges:\n        if not isinstance(edge, list) or len(edge) != 2:\n            valid = False\n            break\n        u, v = edge\n        if type(u) is not int or type(v) is not int or not (0 <= u < n and 0 <= v < n) or u == v:\n            valid = False\n            break\n        pairs.append(frozenset((u, v)))\n    valid = valid and len(set(pairs)) == len(edges)\nif valid:\n    # Connectivity is checked by closure of the binary adjacency relation.\n    connected = [[a == b for b in range(n)] for a in range(n)]\n    for u, v in edges:\n        connected[u][v] = connected[v][u] = True\n    for k in range(n):\n        for a in range(n):\n            for b in range(n):\n                connected[a][b] = connected[a][b] or (connected[a][k] and connected[k][b])\n    valid = all(all(row) for row in connected)\n\nif not valid:\n    error = {'error': 'precondition'}\n    if got != error or expected != error:\n        reject()\n    raise SystemExit(0)\n\n# Independent exhaustive search: no leaf pruning and no candidate imports.\nsolutions = []\nfor assignment in itertools.product(range(3), repeat=len(edges)):\n    divergence = [0] * n\n    for value, (tail, head) in zip(assignment, edges):\n        divergence[tail] -= value\n        divergence[head] += value\n    if all(divergence[v] % 3 == r[v] for v in range(n)):\n        solutions.append(list(assignment))\nif len(solutions) != 1:\n    reject()\nif not isinstance(got, list) or len(got) != len(edges):\n    reject()\nif any(type(value) is not int or value not in (0, 1, 2) for value in got):\n    reject()\nif not isinstance(expected, list) or any(type(value) is not int for value in expected):\n    reject()\nif got != solutions[0] or expected != solutions[0]:\n    reject()\nraise SystemExit(0)\n",

    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON object with n, oriented edges [tail, head], and vertex demands r.\n# PRECONDITIONS: A nonempty finite tree; demands are residues modulo 3 and sum to zero modulo demands, degrees, and an edge output list.\n# ARITHMETIC: Exact integer arithmetic reduced modulo 3.\n# FAILURE: Malformed input or violated preconditions produces a precondition error.\nimport json\nimport sys\nfrom collections import deque\n\n\ndef solve(data):\n    fail = {'error': 'precondition'}\n    if not isinstance(data, dict) or set(data) != {'n', 'edges', 'r'}:\n        return fail\n    n, edges, demand = data['n'], data['edges'], data['r']\n    if type(n) is not int or n < 1:\n        return fail\n    if not isinstance(edges, list) or len(edges) != n - 1:\n        return fail\n    if not isinstance(demand, list) or len(demand) != n:\n        return fail\n    if any(type(a) is not int or not 0 <= a < 3 for a in demand):\n        return fail\n    if sum(demand) % 3 != 0:\n        return fail\n    adj = [[] for _ in range(n)]\n    seen_edges = set()\n    for idx, edge in enumerate(edges):\n        if not isinstance(edge, list) or len(edge) != 2:\n            return fail\n        u, v = edge\n        if type(u) is not int or type(v) is not int:\n            return fail\n        if not (0 <= u < n and 0 <= v < n) or u == v:\n            return fail\n        key = tuple(sorted((u, v)))\n        if key in seen_edges:\n            return fail\n        seen_edges.add(key)\n        adj[u].append((v, idx))\n        adj[v].append((u, idx))\n    reached = {0}\n    stack = [0]\n    while stack:\n        u = stack.pop()\n        for v, _ in adj[u]:\n            if v not in reached:\n                reached.add(v)\n                stack.append(v)\n    if len(reached) != n:\n        return fail\n    need = demand[:]\n    degree = [len(row) for row in adj]\n    active = [True] * len(edges)\n    queue = deque(v for v in range(n) if degree[v] == 1)\n    z = [0] * len(edges)\n    remaining = len(edges)\n    while queue:\n        v = queue.popleft()\n        if degree[v] != 1:\n            continue\n        w, idx = next((w, idx) for w, idx in adj[v] if active[idx])\n        eps = -1 if edges[idx][0] == v else 1\n        z[idx] = (eps * need[v]) % 3\n        need[w] = (need[w] + need[v]) % 3\n        need[v] = 0\n        active[idx] = False\n        remaining -= 1\n        degree[v] -= 1\n        degree[w] -= 1\n        if degree[w] == 1:\n            queue.append(w)\n    if remaining != 0 or any(need):\n        return fail\n    return z\n\n\ntry:\n    result = solve(json.load(sys.stdin))\nexcept (ValueError, TypeError, KeyError, StopIteration):\n    result = {'error': 'precondition'}\nprint(json.dumps(result, separators=(',', ':')))\n",

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The configuration-deletion procedure gives a concrete consistency checker for finite relational inputs whose remaining compatibility constraints follow a spanning tree. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The tree divergence construction solves prescribed finite-field equations; it supplies no game rules, temporal dynamics, or simulation accuracy contract. |
| tools | True | moderate | implementable | inferred | The automorphism filter supplies an explicit validator for proposed permutation witnesses on a finite relational input, including preservation of selected atoms. |
| algorithms | True | strong | implementable | inferred | Leaf elimination constructs the unique edge assignment realizing a zero-sum divergence demand on an arbitrarily oriented finite tree over the stated field. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Forms and molecules represent supported hereditary objects, but the representation has unrestricted depth and depends on realized counting types. The paper gives no general storage or indexing performance contract for this representation. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The resource proof bounds expanded logical evaluation and temporary hereditary objects. It does not construct a circuit, memory architecture, or accelerator execution scheme. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The central extension and its faithful automorphism action establish algebraic symmetry properties, without a cryptographic primitive, adversary model, or security reduction. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Eventual indistinguishability is proved for fixed sentences of the specified choiceless formalism. No learning architecture, training procedure, or statistical generalization claim is constructed. |
| optimisation_operations_research | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The tree test can be adapted to finite constraint feasibility when each vertex has a finite configuration domain and adjacent tree vertices must agree on an incident edge state. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Witnesses preserve the history of a logical choice computation through full-input automorphisms. The construction specifies no communication protocol, fault model, or distributed agreement mechanism. |
| other (Finite model theory and descriptive complexity) | True | strong | adaptable | inferred | The charged finite structures provide explicit separating instances: the witnessed-choice sentence distinguishes their charge assignments, while every fixed full choiceless sentence eventually agrees on the paired structures. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-expressive-separation-is-not-capture** (formal_scope): Strict expressive separation does not establish capture of all polynomial-time queries or determine the expressive effect of nested witnessed-choice operators. Blocked use: ['Compile arbitrary polynomial-time queries into the constructed witnessed-choice sentence.', 'Infer that nesting witnessed-choice operators adds no expressive power.']
- **limit-fixed-query-and-vocabulary** (formal_scope): The separation concerns a fixed sentence and polynomial annotation over the specified binary vocabulary. Blocked use: ['Transfer the same separation to an arbitrary vocabulary without an encoding argument.', 'Change the separating syntax or annotation with the input size while claiming the stated fixed-sentence theorem.']
- **limit-witnesses-preserve-full-history** (hypothesis): Choice witnesses must preserve the entire relational input, the parameters, and every preceding state, and must connect every ordered pair of candidates. Blocked use: ['Certify a choice using transformations that preserve only local incidence.', 'Preserve the current state setwise without establishing the required history stabilization.']
- **limit-failure-propagation** (edge_case): Failed witnessing or excessive witnessed-choice resources produce a propagating failure value; a surrounding Boolean expression does not convert that failure to false. Blocked use: ['Treat an unsuccessful witnessed-choice call as ordinary rejection inside a comprehension.', 'Identify total Boolean evaluation with the true-model convention alone.']
- **limit-structural-check-and-resolving-parameters** (hypothesis): The all-input sentence returns false when the input check fails or the resolving-parameter set is empty. Successful evaluation therefore does not assert that every input has a usable block order. Blocked use: ['Assume a resolving parameter exists on every finite relational input.', 'Interpret rejection of malformed inputs as a grid-charge decision.']
- **limit-filter-compositions-before-use** (hypothesis): Raw local and cycle relations need not be functions. Only filtered compositions are certified as full-input automorphisms fixing selected atoms. Blocked use: ['Apply a raw relation as an invertible witness without checking it.', 'Omit preservation of nonrelations or of an input relation from the automorphism filter.']
- **limit-guarded-progress-is-grid-specific** (hypothesis): Real-choice progress on every unprocessed edge is established for compatible prefixes on the charged grids. On arbitrary inputs, the guard can instead supply the stabilizing dummy choice. Blocked use: ['Infer that every checked input processes all non-tree edges.', 'Replace guarded choice by unconditional selection from locally compatible candidates.']
- **limit-tree-completion-preconditions** (hypothesis): The completion equivalence requires the structural check, a resolving parameter, and a complete selection outside the associated spanning tree. Blocked use: ['Apply configuration deletion as an exact completion test for an incomplete selection.', 'Use the tree argument as a general feasibility decision procedure for cyclic residual constraints.']
- **limit-tree-divergence-zero-total-and-acyclicity** (hypothesis): The unique divergence solution is asserted for a finite tree over the specified field with zero-total vertex demand. Circulations on cycles prevent extending the uniqueness claim to general graphs. Blocked use: ['Return a feasible flow for nonzero-total demand.', 'Claim a unique solution on a graph with cycles.']
- **limit-resource-polynomial-after-fixed-expansion** (formal_scope): The resource guarantee is tied to a fixed capture-avoiding macro expansion and covers temporary objects before filtering as well as individual choice paths. Blocked use: ['Claim practical efficiency merely from the existence of the polynomial annotation.', 'Collect all terminal choice paths into a single object and assume the same resource proof applies.']
- **limit-small-orbit-required-for-support** (hypothesis): The rank-independent support estimate requires a polynomial bound on the object's orbit under the full automorphism group used in the construction. Blocked use: ['Assign short central supports to arbitrary hereditary objects without bounding their full-group orbits.', 'Replace the full-group orbit hypothesis by a central-group orbit hypothesis without further proof.']
- **limit-hereditary-family-conditions** (hypothesis): Hereditary support for every recursive member requires a finite invariant membership-transitive family satisfying the cardinality bound. The individual supports need not coincide. Blocked use: ['Infer hereditary support from a support of the root alone.', 'Assume a single short tuple simultaneously supports the whole evaluation family.']
- **limit-box-boundary-regime** (hypothesis): The stated boundary estimate applies to nonempty vertex subsets of the specified box containing at most half its vertices. Blocked use: ['Use the same bound on an arbitrary graph.', 'Apply the displayed estimate directly to larger vertex subsets.']
- **limit-eventual-width-and-fixed-parameters** (hypothesis): Base equivalence and the support-scale width comparison hold for sufficiently large boxes after the sentence parameters have been fixed. Blocked use: ['Assert the eventual counting equivalence for every permitted box size.', 'Allow the fixed sentence parameters to grow with box size while invoking the same asymptotic comparison.', 'Replace sentence-dependent eventual thresholds by a common threshold for all CPT sentences.']
- **limit-partial-flow-component-balance** (hypothesis): Prescribed edge shifts extend to a circulation exactly when every component remaining after deletion satisfies its balance condition. Blocked use: ['Check only the sum of all component demands and infer extendibility.', 'Accept a prescribed shift despite an unbalanced remaining component.']
- **limit-analytical-expansion-not-input** (formal_scope): The auxiliary preorder and raw scalar-difference relations belong to the lower-bound analysis and are not supplied to the choiceless program. The larger automorphism group is required only on the original input. Blocked use: ['Give the analytical label order to the separating computation as an input relation.', 'Assume the larger automorphism group preserves the auxiliary expansion.']
- **limit-transfer-needs-homogeneity-and-width** (hypothesis): Supported-domain counting transfer requires nonempty finite bases of the stated relational arity, base counting equivalence, the orbit-homogeneity hypothesis, and the width inequality. Blocked use: ['Infer supported-domain equivalence from base sentence equivalence alone.', "Drop nonemptiness while retaining the proof's support-padding argument.", 'Apply the theorem to unrestricted relational arity without a separate argument.']
- **limit-supported-domains-are-infinite** (nonconstructive): The supported domains are countably infinite despite the finite hereditary size of each object. Their proof bijections are mathematical witnesses; no definability of those bijections is asserted. Blocked use: ['Enumerate the entire supported domain as a finite verification instance.', 'Treat the domain bijection as a definable canonization algorithm.']
- **limit-evaluation-trace-includes-rejected-candidates** (hypothesis): The trace used for support and evaluation transfer includes rejected candidates and the extra candidate needed for the final stationarity test. Blocked use: ['Discard excessive candidates before forming the trace used in the lower-bound argument.', 'Stop recording values before the final stationarity comparison.']
- **limit-description-only-at-actual-arguments** (formal_scope): Term-graph correctness is required at actual activations, with output uniqueness over the entire ambient domain. No correctness assertion is made for argument tuples that never occur. Blocked use: ['Use the description as an evaluator on arbitrary assignments.', 'Permit unrelated proposed outputs to create iteration continuations or suppress the empty default.']
- **limit-description-domain-and-formula-length** (hypothesis): The evaluation description requires a membership-transitive domain containing all atoms, all pure hereditary sets, and the complete actual trace. Fixed variable width does not imply polynomial formula length or internal constructibility by the CPT sentence. Blocked use: ['Evaluate the description in a truncated domain missing descendants or pure sets.', 'Infer an efficient compiler or uniform polynomial-length translation from the fixed-width result.']
- **limit-finite-oracle-covers-only-tree-ingredient** (formal_scope): Finite tree-divergence checks concern a constructive ingredient and do not certify the full expressive separation, which also requires supported-domain transfer and eventual equivalence for every fixed CPT sentence. Blocked use: ['Promote successful finite flow checks to verification of the full lower bound.', 'Treat finite test coverage as a proof of all witnessed-choice histories on arbitrary inputs.']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 243 (`lean/docs/243.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Choiceless polynomial time with counting does not capture polynomial time](../../preprints/Choiceless-polynomial-time-with-counting-does-not-capture-polynomial-time-September-23-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Witnessed symmetric choice is strictly stronger than choiceless polynomial time with counting](../../preprints/Witnessed-symmetric-choice-is-strictly-stronger-than-choiceless-polynomial-time-with-counting-September-24-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalized result separates polynomial time from choiceless polynomial time with counting. It gives an explicit query on finite structures with eight relations that is invariant under isomorphism and decidable in polynomial time, but is not definable in the stated hereditarily finite-set language with cardinality and polynomial bounds on stages and intermediate objects. The query applies to all finite inputs without a promise. The separate witnessed-symmetric-choice result is not included.
>
> The formalization proves that witnessed symmetric choice strictly increases the expressive power of choiceless polynomial time with counting. It constructs one fixed sentence with exactly one witnessed-choice occurrence that gives a Boolean answer on every finite input, while no sentence of the original counting formalism defines the same class of inputs.
